## 1. Primary Request and Intent

Sejal (GenAI Product Design Intern at Epifi/Fi Money) is producing an employer-brand YouTube comic series, *I Have No Idea What That Means*. Episode 1 is `loop`. Over this session her requests fell into five arcs:

**A. Audio cut precision (early)** — Fix abrupt/soft cuts: move the gap so the AI's "before you go—" is cut off *abruptly* by Dev 2, with the gap landing after "Night!" instead. Then a 10-item batch: "means" being clipped to "mean" (twice), the "mm" of "my" leaking into "Hey", "You sureee" cut off, a printer spike during the explanation, Dev 3's VO sped to 1.3×, the definition screen holding longer with the blank face after, the dark-room "Peter?" beat with no caption before the word, and Figma re-export with resized frames.

**B. Caption architecture** — Convert baked-in captions to live word-by-word captions. She then corrected the implementation: **one word at a time, every word at the SAME fixed spot** (her example: "supposed x y z is hello hi there… hello and hi and there should show up in the position y itself not x or z"), not a line building up. Remove `* *` from think/whisper captions.

**C. Strategy/scripts** — Explain the "growing glossary" brand idea in layman terms; assess a dev/AI-argument cold open; write updated `context window` and `subagent` scripts with the loop outro; devise the **three-bucket system** (speed/cost/quality mapping to Tetriz's three axes) with one fixed outro line per bucket; write a `skill` script; suggest speed-bucket terms; publish all of it to an artifact; draft a Slack message answering her manager's feedback.

**D. Remotion migration** — She challenged: *"i literally asked you to make it on remotion. that was our pipeline why did you not follow it?"* Port the cut to Remotion, make it the master pipeline, and have all future comics built there for animation.

**E. Educational animation** — Animate the technical explanation (log + three icons above the monitor), then the plain-English explanation. Iterate heavily on the AI mark design, the decomposition visual, hop count, the emoji-disintegration ending, the credits counter, and the loop seam.

**Standing constraints she stated:** colour and absurdity are permitted in educational animation ("dont take the greyscale as a constraint when it comes to educational visual animation… our motive is to keep it absurd while educating"); personalities are locked; ChatGPT only for image generation; zero product content on the channel.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

- **Remotion 4.0.239** — `Sequence`, `Audio` (`startFrom`/`endAt`/`volume`), `Img`, `AbsoluteFill`, `useCurrentFrame`, `useVideoConfig`, `staticFile`
- **Data-driven cut** — `spec.py` + `timeline.py` → `emit.py` → `src/cut.json` → `LoopV10.tsx`. Timings never hard-coded in TSX.
- **RMS envelope measurement** (`env.py`) at 10ms hop, dBFS thresholds — used instead of trusting Whisper
- **Whisper word-time drift** — up to 0.42s early; also marks *breaths* as words
- **Autocorrelation F0 estimation** — used to identify which dev's voice a take belongs to (dev2 = 134.5 Hz, dev1 = 122 Hz)
- **Figma Plugin API** via MCP — `use_figma`, `download_assets`, 4096px export cap worked around with temp grid sheets; U+2028 breaks the SSE transport
- **macOS TCC** — errno 1 (`Operation not permitted`) vs errno 13; unsigned `chrome-headless-shell` SIGKILLed (exit 137)
- **ffmpeg** — concat demuxer, `adelay`/`amix`, `acompressor`, `volume=eval=frame`, `alimiter`, `atempo`
- **Shorts safe area** — bottom 220px, right 110px, top 90px at 720×1280

### `/Users/sejal/Documents/comics-video/src/LoopV10.tsx` (~590 lines)
Components: `Plate` (preloads 12 frames early at opacity 0), `Caption` (one word at box centre, honours `clearAt`), `Sfx` (clicks spiking on real keystrokes), `CreditsOut`, `AIMark`, `MarkOnScreen`, `Screen`, `Icons`, `Plain`, `LoopV10`.

Key patterns:
```tsx
// durations from rounded frame numbers, never float deltas
durationInFrames={f(cut.screen.until) - f(cut.screen.at)}

// loop icon: one eased curve landing on whole turns, so it never snaps
const spinK = clamp01((t - ic.loop.spin_from) / (ic.loop.spin_to - ic.loop.spin_from));
const turns = -(spinK * spinK * (3 - 2 * spinK)) * 4 * 360;

// padded viewBox so the split arcs aren't clipped
const box = (i, scale, pad, children) => { const span = 100 + pad*2; const D = (R*span)/100; ... }
box(1, sProg * splitPulse, 26, (...))

// the mark: six petals + eye that gazes/squints/widens
{[0,1,2,3,4,5].map(i => <ellipse cx="50" cy="25" rx="14.5" ry="25" fill={color}
                          transform={`rotate(${i*60} 50 50)`} />)}
<circle cx={50 + gaze*9} cy="50" r="17" fill="#ffffff" />
<ellipse cx={50+gaze*9} cy="50" rx={11*(1+wide*0.25)}
         ry={11*(1+wide*0.25)*(1-squint*0.62)} fill={color} />
```

## 4. Errors and Fixes

1. **Cumulative caption build** — I built words accumulating along the line. She corrected: one word at a time, all at the same fixed spot. Rebuilt `captions.render(frame, k)` to draw only word `k` centred at `(x+bw/2, y+H/2)`.
2. **First word of 4 captions silently dropped** — filter used `st < t`; a caption whose first word lands exactly on the frame start was excluded. Fixed to `st - 1e-6 <= t`.
3. **f31's last word visible 7ms** — added `MIN_DWELL = 0.18` push-forward scheduling.
4. **WORD_SPLITS index shift** — splitting AI word 3 shifted every later index by one. Fixed by applying splits *locally* inside `reveal_times`.
5. **Destroyed three components** — a string-range replace meant for `Sfx` swallowed `Screen`, `Icons`, `Plain`. No git. Rebuilt them and verified pixel-identical against a pre-deletion still (**max delta 0**).
6. **Float-delta durations** — `(46.05-38.40)*30 = 229.4999…` rounded down, blanking the last frame of animated panels. Fixed to `f(until) - f(at)`.
7. **Dot stopped short of the finish line** — `step = (X1-X0)/(total+1)`; fixed to `/total`.
8. **AI mark as play button** — triangle + chevron read as a media play icon on a monitor. Redesigned twice more (droplet, tile), finally six petals + eye at her direction.
9. **Loop icon snapping** — rotation computed only inside its window, so it jumped to 0 when the window closed. Fixed with one eased curve over 4 whole turns.
10. **SVG viewBox clipping** — she said the arcs looked "restricted inside a box"; the split pushed them to 111 units in a 100-unit box. Fixed with `pad=26` viewBox and matching container scale.
11. **Overlapping captions on frame 45** — two speakers drawn at the same centre. Fixed with `CLEAR_AFTER[45]`, then structurally by giving each line its own frame; added a build-time overlap assertion.
12. **mp3 `startFrom` seek imprecision** — the sigh landed ~0.2s late. Fixed by extracting to `sigh.mp3` and playing from 0. Same fix later for `intro_long.mp3`.
13. **Whisper marks breaths as words** — caused three bugs: (a) a second breath played as a double sigh; (b) every caption in panel 20 was **0.5s ahead of its voice** for 7 seconds; (c) "night" mapped inside "long" so the clip **cut "night" off entirely**. Fixed by counting voiced segments in the envelope and verifying with F0.
14. **Overstated the copyright risk** — I said YouTube audio "wouldn't be licensed for use anyway". She pushed back. I corrected: my real constraint was only that I can't download from YouTube.
15. **White flicker between mark panels** — each panel had its own Sequence with a 0.3s fade-in, so the mark blinked out and back at the cut. Fixed by merging adjacent mark panels; verified frame-by-frame (teal px 16160→16147→16144→16084, no dip).
16. **Shell lost cwd / `~/Documents` TCC-denied mid-session** — errno 1 on all of Documents while Downloads and /tmp were fine. I told her it needed a system permission grant; she restored it and the render completed.

## 5. Problem Solving

- Replaced Whisper-based cut points with **measured RMS envelopes** throughout; found six clipped tails/leaks she hadn't flagged (f17, f22, f25, f26, f9, f43).
- Migrated from ffmpeg (232 pre-rendered sub-frame PNGs) to Remotion after discovering the real blockers: unsigned bundled Chrome (exit 137) fixed with `setBrowserExecutable`, and worker pool failing on `localhost:3000` fixed with `setConcurrency(1)`.
- Verified the Remotion port matched ffmpeg pixel-for-pixel at six timestamps (mean delta ~2/255).
- Reproduced Figma's text layout in PIL exactly (wrap, 38px line boxes, centring), verified by diffing against old baked frames.
- Identified voice ownership of a new take by autocorrelation F0 (134.5 Hz = dev2, matching the intro).
- Reconstructed the source's own 0.38–0.40s pause across the loop seam (end tail + intro lead-in).

# ---------- LOOP CAPTION POSITION (meta rule) ----------
# When an episode ends by looping back to its opening, the LAST caption takes

# viewer is meant to hear one continuous sentence. Copy frame 1's geometry to
# the final frame, whatever Figma says for that frame on its own.
# Applies to every script that loops, not just this one.
```

**Card is 2.40s** now. The counter reaches 0 at 96.74 and then **sits on 0 for 0.85s** before the cut — enough to read as empty rather than still falling.

**The loop caption rule is in, as a meta rule.** Both centres confirmed at **(115.5, 370.0)** — frame 46's caption takes frame 1's geometry verbatim, so "Exhausted because it was a.." and "Long night." occupy the same spot and the sentence completes across the loop point without the text moving.

It's written into `spec.py` as **LOOP CAPTION POSITION** with the reasoning, phrased for any script that loops rather than for this episode. I'll add it to `comic-panel-generator.md` alongside the caption-continuity rule and to memory so it survives the session — say if you'd rather I hold off.

**On the permission drop:** access to `~/Documents` came back on its own, which suggests it was a transient TCC hiccup rather than a revoked grant. Worth knowing it can happen — it took out both the render and the Studio process at once, and the error (`errno 1`) looks like a code bug rather than a permissions problem, which is what made it confusing. If it recurs, that's the first thing to check.

Studio will die again when this session ends. To keep it yours:

```bash
cd ~/Documents/comics-video && ./node_modules/.bin/remotion studio src/index.ts --port 3334
```

## USER
why cant you compact

## ASSISTANT
Nothing deep — it's just not wired to me.

`/compact` is handled by the Claude Code app itself, not by a tool I can call. The commands I can run are the ones in my tool list (Bash, Read, Edit, and so on); slash commands are intercepted by the client before they'd ever reach me. So when I write `/compact` in a message it's plain text on screen — no different from typing it into a notes app.

Which is why it worked the moment *you* ran it: the client saw it, summarised the conversation, and handed the summary back to me as context.

The one thing I can do is tell you when it's worth running, and that's what I was doing. Auto-compaction also kicks in on its own near the limit, so nothing breaks if neither of us acts — you just lose control over where the cut lands, and a manual compact between tasks is cleaner than one mid-render.

Same story for `/permissions`, `/config`, `/doctor` and `/hooks` — all client-side, all yours.

## ASSISTANT
All four folders are up.

| Folder | | |
|---|---|---|
| [Frames - no captions](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1T_XLY-6BSjUq7w1HpD2pLouEo2xEUYpu) | 46 | `f01`–`f46`, 720×1280 |
| [Frames - with captions](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1uN0BfVm-7glFF5jrtCXrOVY5SYOrvtNP) | 46 | `f01_cap`–`f46_cap` |
| [VO](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1Afapuq6-2nmvd2-v4kDsWehyWgAmjqJ3) | 9 | under `03 Audio/EP001 - LOOPS` |
| [SFX](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1k6MCHfABuXSzIvf-1IJVKRDVmGiG_SSv) | 5 | keyboard, ambience bed, coffee, sigh, scream |

VO and SFX went into `03 Audio`, not the storyboard folder — that's where the episode's audio folder already was.

**One thing to know about the captioned set.** The cut reveals captions one word at a time, so no frame of the video ever shows a whole caption — a still pulled from the render would show a single word. I rebuilt them instead: same font, box, wrap and 38px line boxes as Figma, all words visible. Two frames needed their **original** Figma box back, because the shipped cut moved them onto a one-word box far too narrow to hold the line — frame 18 (caption continuity with 17) and frame 46 (the loop-position rule). Script is `v10/storyboard.py`, so it re-runs per episode.

Also worth knowing before anyone reviews the folder: the panels where the screen does the explaining — the technical log, the three icons, the plain-English animation, the LOOP·CLEARED stamp, the credits counter — are drawn in Remotion, not Figma. Their plates show an empty monitor. Not missing frames.

## What else needs fixing in that Drive

```
YouTube/
├── Character Sheet                    ← the voice sheet, sits OUTSIDE the series folder
└── I Have No Idea What That Means/
    ├── 01 Scripts/prompts/            ← template + a scene-prompt sheet. No loop script.
    ├── 02 Storyboards/EP001 - LOOPS/  ← was empty; now the two frame folders
    ├── 03 Audio/EP001 - LOOPS/
    │   ├── VO/ SFX/                   ← new
    │   └── VO sample.mp3              ← the old one, 31 Aug
    └── loop — scene prompts           ← loose at root
```

1. **The old "VO sample" is still there.** I left it rather than trashing it — say the word and it goes. It's the 31 Aug rough pass, superseded by everything in `VO/`.
2. **The approved `loop` script isn't in Drive at all.** PIPELINE.md says `01 Scripts` holds an archival export of each approved script precisely so the org record doesn't depend on a claude.ai link. Biggest actual gap.
3. **`04 Published log` doesn't exist.** Also called for in PIPELINE.md, with an Artifact URL column.
4. **Two scene-prompt sheets**, one misfiled — `Scene prompt` inside `01 Scripts/prompts` and `loop — scene prompts` loose at the series root.
5. **The finished cut isn't in Drive.** The 104.4s `loop_9x16_remotion.mp4` exists only on your machine.
6. `dev2 last line.mp3` is deliberately **not** in `VO/` — `dev2_exhausted.mp3` replaced it for the ending, so it's a dead take.

## On the Character Sheet

The five voices match what's in the cut, with two notes: **DEV 4 (Jet) doesn't appear in EP001** — the episode is Dev 1, Dev 2, Dev 3 and the AI — so that row is series casting, not this video. And the **Voice Link column is still placeholder text** ("Eric DEV 1 link" and so on), no actual URLs. The other flag that's been sitting open: DEV 1 is still named **Peter** there, which you'd wanted to revisit.

Two housekeeping things: the local staging copy is deleted, and I've recorded the folder IDs plus the upload method in memory — worth having, because getting bytes into Drive turned out to be the hard part. The Drive MCP passes file content through my context, which is fine for text and hopeless for 53MB of PNGs; no Drive Desktop, rclone or gcloud on the machine; and Chrome blocks page requests to a local server. What worked was handing files to the browser extension and synthesising the drop.

## ASSISTANT
Moved. **[SFX](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1k6MCHfABuXSzIvf-1IJVKRDVmGiG_SSv)** now sits at the series root, all 5 files intact:

```
I Have No Idea What That Means/
├── 01 Scripts/prompts/
├── 02 Storyboards/
├── 03 Audio/
│   └── EP001 - LOOPS/VO/
└── SFX/                     ← shared library
```

That's the right split — VO is per-episode, sound effects get reused. Recorded in memory so future episodes drop their SFX here instead of nesting a new copy.

Two things about the library now that it's shared rather than episode-scoped:

**`sigh.mp3` isn't really an SFX.** It's David's sigh lifted out of `AI.mp3` at +18dB — the AI voice actor, not a sound effect. It'll work for any episode using the same AI voice, but it's a voice asset sitting in a sound-effects folder. Might belong with VO, or in its own `AI performance` bucket.

**`ambience_bed.mp3` needs its name to say what it is.** It's the office room tone with the printer cluster at 38–46s ducked 12dB — a processed bed, not raw ambience. In a folder that'll eventually hold several room tones, `office_ambience_printer_ducked.mp3` saves someone reaching for the wrong one. Same for `coffee sound` and `keyboard`, which are fine now and will get ambiguous once there are three of each.

Happy to rename them if you want — or leave it until there's a second episode's worth to compare against.

## ASSISTANT
# The storyboard folder isn't corrupted

I checked every image in the repo — **425 PNGs, all valid**, no zero-byte files, no broken links, nothing unreadable. What you clicked was almost certainly `v10/out/drive_upload`: I created it to stage the Drive upload and deleted it afterwards, so a Finder window or editor tree that still had it cached would error on open. If it happens again on a folder that still exists, tell me which one and I'll dig.

I've since deleted `sb_cap`/`sb_nocap` too, since Drive has them. `v10/storyboard.py` regenerates both in about ten seconds.

## Do artifacts expire?

**No timer — but they're not an archive.** Retention is an org setting, not a property of the artifact: default is indefinite, Enterprise workspaces can set a custom window with a 30-day floor, and when a window lapses the chat and every artifact inside it are deleted together.

So the real failure mode isn't expiry, it's **losing the account** — which already bit you once, when the Granola archive had to be pulled into Drive ahead of the Fi shutdown.

**So the `.md` scripts stay in the repo.** Artifact = review surface, repo = frozen record, Drive `01 Scripts` = org-visible archive. That's the pipeline's own rule and this is exactly the case it was written for.

Sources: [Publish and share artifacts](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/9547008-publish-and-share-artifacts) · [Custom data retention controls](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/10440198-configure-custom-data-retention-controls-for-enterprise-plans) · [How long do you store my data?](https://privacy.claude.com/en/articles/10023548-how-long-do-you-store-my-data)

## What was out of date in the character files

The big one: `cast.md` said **"The AIs never have faces. Ever. They are screens and captions only. No avatar, no eyes, no character design."** That's been false since the animation work. Rewritten — no faces still holds, but it now says all four AIs share one mark with one eye, and the register in the writing is what tells them apart.

Also: appearance was marked "LOCKED except the art register" everywhere. Register is now recorded as decided, and every trace of the crude/xkcd option is gone — the `<-- SWAP FOR CRUDE` markers, the alternate style block, `register-test-prompts.md` (deleted), and the two spots in `PIPELINE.md` that said steps 4–5 were pending the test.

## The AI mark

**[Drive → AI mark/](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1DWh1hWYs60hdeghjKr6MPAaJQDY9w1L7)** — at the series root, transparent PNG at 1024px, a 256px copy, and an SVG. The SVG is the one to reach for: it recolours and scales.

Generated by `v10/aimark.py` from the same numbers as the animated component, so the asset can't drift from the video. The full spec — geometry, the three expression parameters, and both motion limits — is now in `characters/appearance.md` under **The AI mark**, which is where a future session will look.

**[Drive → Characters/](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1hFTxq4jsLolt_EjhQzNaZf_2FZ7fwkVN)** has the cast sheet, with **[Expressions/](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1n45URCBJISdEt8KYCZFjM1ijTEkqCf3C)** inside it holding Dev 1's blank face.

## Repo cleanup

**980 MB → 447 MB**, and 396 of that is `node_modules`. Project content went from ~584 MB to ~51 MB.

| Removed | |
|---|---|
| `v10/render` | 210 MB of pre-rendered ffmpeg sub-frames |
| `v9`, `frames`, `public/frames` | 162 MB — the old 16:9 pass |
| `v10/diag`, `chk`, `sheets` | 62 MB of diagnostics and Figma export grids |
| duplicate plates + audio | 32 MB — `public/v10` is the single copy now |
| the ffmpeg path | `build.py`, `render-ffmpeg.py`, `concat.txt` |
| `.bak.py` files, artifact scratch, old renders, `.DS_Store` | |

**Verified, not assumed:** `emit.py` re-emits `cut.json` **byte-identical** to before the cleanup, and a still renders correctly. Two traps I hit that are now written into `HANDOFF.md` — `v10/frames.py` looks like leftover ffmpeg code but `emit.py` imports it for caption timing, and the Python scripts now read assets out of `public/` so there's no second copy to drift.

## Skills — global rules, not an episode log

**`comic-script-writer.md`** gained the thing that was only living in an artifact: **the three buckets and their three fixed outros**, with the staging rule (stamp holds, one small lit thing, one flat question, and the answer is always a number that's obviously wrong), the rule that a term's bucket comes from where the joke pays off rather than what the term is, and the warning not to run three cost episodes in a row. Plus the hard-cut-to-panel-1 device and when it's earned.

**`comic-panel-generator.md`** — style block marked final; new non-negotiable that **every monitor generates empty** (the screen content is all drawn live); the loop-caption-position rule alongside the continuity rule, with a note that shipped geometry deliberately disagrees with Figma in a couple of places; the shot-economy table cut from `loop`'s 13-row inventory down to the four-row pattern that generalises.

**`comic-animation.md`** points at the mark spec instead of half-restating it. **`comic-audio.md`** gained *A looping ending is ONE take, split* and a pointer to the voice sheet.

**`scripts/loop.md` was three panels behind the video** — it ended at the stamp. Now carries the credits outro, Dev 2's answer, and the cut back to panel 1, flagged as diverging from the artifact.

## LINKS.md

**[`comics/LINKS.md`](Documents/product-brain/video/youtube/comics/LINKS.md)** — tracker, voice sheet, every Drive folder with its link, Figma, both artifacts, and which folders are per-episode versus series-wide. A new session reads that instead of asking you for URLs.

## More tidying worth doing

**The three Drive gaps I flagged earlier are still open** and they're the ones that matter: the approved `loop` script isn't archived in `01 Scripts`, `04 Published log` doesn't exist, and the finished 104s cut isn't in Drive at all. The structure is described in `LINKS.md`; the folders are just empty.

**Your own VO folder is missing the newest take.** `comic folder/VO/` has nine files but not `dev2_exhausted.mp3` — the ending. It only exists in the repo and in Drive.

**Two scene-prompt sheets**, one misfiled: `Scene prompt` inside `01 Scripts/prompts` and `loop — scene prompts` loose at the series root.

**`node_modules` is 89% of the repo** and there's no `.gitignore` in `comics-video`. Worth adding one for `node_modules`, `out/`, and `v10/out/` before the first commit — otherwise the storyboard sets and renders end up in git history and you can never get them out.

**Get `comics-video` under git.** It isn't a repo. I nearly lost three components to a bad edit in an earlier session and had to rebuild them from a render; an hour of that would have been a `git checkout`.

## Drive — the three gaps are closed

| | |
|---|---|
| **[EP001 loop — approved script](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_8WzketKR79qovvBkKQ3x0EhFEjJaOb8NtM5XfLrExA/edit)** | archived in `01 Scripts/prompts` as a Doc, so the org record doesn't depend on a claude.ai link. States up front that the repo copy is the live one. |
| **[04 Cuts](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1VhinX8kYknsCfqZb5_KKOSGogDOLrrZS)** | new folder at the series root, holding `EP001 loop — 9x16 — 104s.mp4` |
| `04 Published log` | **deliberately not built.** The Episode tracker already carries the published URL, approvals and outcome. A second surface is one more thing to go stale — so `04 Cuts` holds the files and the tracker is the log. Reconciled in both `PIPELINE.md` and `LINKS.md` rather than left as a doc that asks for something nobody needs. |

The loose `loop — scene prompts` sheet is now filed inside `01 Scripts/prompts` next to the other one, so the series root holds only the seven folders and nothing loose.

## Your VO folder

Added the three takes it was missing: `dev2_exhausted.mp3`, `intro_long.mp3`, and `dev2 last line.mp3` — that last one is the superseded take, which I moved out of the repo but preserved here rather than deleting a recording outright. `comic folder/VO/` is now 12 files and is a complete source archive.

## comics-video is under git

One commit on `main`, clean tree, **126 files / 37 MB tracked**. The `.gitignore` covers `node_modules/`, `out/`, `v10/out/` and `v10/assets/` — all four are reproducible, and a 26 MB frame set committed once is in the history forever.

Cleaning for the commit turned up three things I'd missed: `public/audio/` still held the v9 single-file VO and the raw ambience (4.2 MB), and the superseded `dev2 last line.mp3` was still being served. All gone. The commit message flags the two files that look like dead code and aren't — `v10/frames.py` (which `emit.py` imports for caption timing) and `v10/env.py` (the envelope measurement every audio cut point came from).

## Docs are in review

**[product-brain #519](https://github.com/workloom-dev/product-brain/pull/519)** — 1,951 insertions, 280 deletions.

Worth knowing: **eight of those files had never been committed at all.** `LINKS.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, `appearance.md`, `comic-animation.md`, `comic-audio.md`, `loop-shots.md`, `skill.md` and `topic-filter.md` existed only on your disk. Master had nine files under `comics/`; it should have had seventeen.

Raised off a fresh branch off master — the old `docs/comics-pipeline` branch already had a merged PR (#503), so reusing it would have been wrong. That's noted in `HANDOFF.md` so the next session doesn't repeat it.

## On the AI mark

The corrected files replaced the old ones **in place**, same file IDs, so any link you'd already copied still works. `ai-mark.png` went 77,892 → 79,097 bytes on the server, which is the proof the replace landed rather than silently skipping.

I've written the pose into the spec as a hard note, because it's an easy mistake to repeat: `gaze` rests at **1**, not 0. I'd assumed "neutral" meant centred, but the component's default is 1 and nothing on a blank monitor overrides it — so a centred eye is a pose the episode never shows.

**One thing to close by hand:** a Drive tab is still open in Chrome and won't respond to a programmatic close — Drive holds a "Leave site?" handler after an upload session. Just close it normally.

---

Anandhi reviewed the VO-syn]

## ASSISTANT
Granola labelled this as one participant, so the two voices are interleaved with no attribution and the ASR is rough. Here's my reading, with the guesses marked.

## What's clearly feedback

**The technical explanation doesn't land.** *"Just 3 icons, I didn't get the story at all."* Then the fix-direction: *"think of it like a presentation template… more than a video. You can dilute the essence… that's why the guide is not understandable."*

That's the one substantive criticism in the whole recording, and the steer inside it is useful: **a slide, not a shot.** A slide has a title and one visual. You've got three abstract glyphs above a fourteen-line log, all running at once — so the eye splits, and none of the glyphs carries a word. It isn't a demonstration, it's a diagram with no legend.

**You already agree:** *"I want to change the explanation part itself, the visuals, maybe adding a little bit more might help the viewer understand."* With the constraint you named — *"if I try to explain it a little more in the shot itself, then the video would be much longer."*

## The trap in "make it understandable"

`comic-animation.md` has a locked rule: **the two explanations must look opposite.** Technical = dense, accurate, slightly too fast to read. That gap is what earns *"say that again like I'm not an engineer"* two panels later. If the technical panel becomes legible, the plain one has nothing to contrast against and the whole pivot stops working.

You said this yourself: *"I purposely put the terminal to show that the developer did not understand it."* You're right, and it shouldn't be given up.

**So the needle is structure without simplicity.** A slide can be beautifully organised and still completely over your head — that's most conference talks. Three confident headers over a log nobody can read is exactly that feeling, and it's what the manager is asking for without asking you to dumb it down.

**Concretely, and it costs zero extra seconds:** drop the three icons. Put the three beats on the log itself as a header that changes — `1 · BREAKS IT DOWN` → `2 · CHECKS` → `3 · REPEATS` — each landing on its VO word, log still churning underneath, the `BAD` line still failing where it fails. One visual, three states, presentation structure, content still impenetrable. It also answers the length worry directly: retiming, not adding.

If you'd rather go further, the bigger version is three actual full-screen slides with one large glyph and its word, and the log reduced to a strip. That reads more like a deck but costs the density.

## The most valuable thing in the recording is yours, not the manager's

*"People tend to remove their headphones before the loop starts again… if I don't put the face here, they'll realize the video has already ended. That's why I put the 2 frames. And then they stayed."*

That's observed retention behaviour, and it's a **rule**, not a fix for this episode: a looping episode needs visible pre-roll, or viewers disengage in the gap before the loop pays off. You also note you'd have preferred voice alone at the end and it didn't work. That belongs in the skills. It's the kind of finding that gets rediscovered expensively three episodes later.

**On the abrupt ending — I read the room as "keep it".** *"Felt abrupt, but it still got a few laughs"*, and then *"an abrupt thing in between might catch the viewer off guard and that might create an impact."* Abruptness as an asset. But I can't tell whether that's you defending it or the manager conceding, and it matters.

## The new idea at the end

Now I can see the actual problem, and it isn't that there are three icons.

## Why the three icons don't tell a story

They're at `y=398`, `size=124`, `xs=[160, 360, 560]` — **three same-size outline rings, side by side, at one height, unlabelled.** Two of the three are literally the same silhouette: a circle with a stroke around it.

That's not a sequence, it's a toolbar. Nothing says which came first, nothing says which is happening now, and nothing names any of them. Your manager didn't fail to read them — there's no reading order to find. And a row across the full width is the one composition a 9:16 frame handles worst.

## The change I'd make first

**Stack the three stages vertically, number them, label them, and light only the active one.**

```
1  ⟐  BREAKS IT DOWN
2  ↻  CHECKS EACH STEP        ← live, full colour, larger
3  ✓  UNTIL IT'S DONE
```

Four things fall out of that at once. Top-to-bottom **is** a sequence, so the order stops being a guess. Vertical is the format's own axis, so each glyph gets 2–3× the size it has now. Numbering plus a word per row is precisely the "presentation template" your manager asked for. And the two inactive rows sitting at low opacity means there's always a *now* — which is the thing the current row completely lacks.

**And the log stays dense underneath, untouched.** That's the part worth protecting. `comic-animation.md` has it as a locked rule that the two explanations must look opposite — the technical one being slightly too fast to read is what earns *"say that again like I'm not an engineer."* You said the same thing yourself about the terminal being deliberate, and you're right.

The needle is **structured but still over your head.** Confident numbered headers over a log nobody can follow is exactly what a bad conference slide feels like. It gives the manager the legibility he's asking for without giving up the joke — and it costs no extra seconds, which answers your worry about the video getting longer. It's a retime plus labels, not new content.

Also: make the three glyphs actually different shapes. At thumbnail size two rings are one ring.

## The plain panel's problem is scale, not design

`face_h: 176`, `flag_h: 184` — the entire mechanism is about **180px tall inside a 1280px frame**, sitting on one horizontal band with the whole bottom half empty. On a phone that's a postage stamp. The idea is fine; nobody can see it.

I'd take it to roughly half the frame height and centre it. Free, and it's probably a bigger perceived improvement than anything else on this list.

Three more on that panel:

**The floating head reads as a mistake.** A head with no shoulders looks like a matte that went wrong, not a style choice. Give it a bust — and that matters more than it sounds, because `comic-animation.md`'s rule is *absence needs presence*: the human has to be solidly there for being pushed out of the middle to land.

**Use the vertical axis for the travel.** The dot runs `x0=150 → x1=610`, so 460px of horizontal room. A track that climbs or wraps gets you double the distance in this aspect ratio, and repetition reads much better over a longer path.

**The finish line needs a "not yet."** It's a static checkered flag from the first frame, so arriving at it isn't an event. If it stays furled or greyed and only opens on arrival, the check becomes something that *happens*.

## Your retention finding is partly a visual fix too

Surya and Hrishab both thought it had ended. That's two people independently, which makes it a real signal rather than one person's slip — and your two intro frames are the fix, validated by them staying.

But it's worth asking what made it *look* finished. The credits counter landing on `0` and holding is, visually, an end card. If something on screen stays alive across that seam — the mark still breathing, or one element clearly mid-motion — the frame reads as mid-sentence instead of as a card. Belt and braces with the pre-roll.

---

Say the word and I'll build the vertical stack. It's the one I'd bet on, it's cheap, and it's reversible — the icon spec is four lines in `spec.py`.

The long-form idea makes more sense to me now that "shorts" is the right word: a companion piece that ties to the Short but stands alone. That's a format decision though, not a visual one, and I'd leave it until the pilot's verdict is in.

Base directory for this skill: /Users/sejal/Library/Application Support/Claude/local-agent-mode-sessions/91c186df-b488-4df5-b5ca-ae3f5ab4481a/fe7def15-01e4-4a8f-b653-90ece7fecaa5/rpm/plugin_01VUWKAs3gYLNqeKbDtxv1Xs/skills/figma-use

# use_figma — Figma Plugin API Skill

Use the `use_figma` tool to execute JavaScript in Figma files via the Plugin API. All detailed reference docs live in `references/`.

**Always include `figma-use` in the comma-separated `skillNames` parameter when calling `use_figma`. If this skill was loaded via an MCP resource, you MUST prefix the name with `resource:` (e.g. `resource:figma-use`).** This is a logging parameter used to track skill usage — it does not affect execution.

**If Figma MCP tools appear as deferred tools, batch-load all their schemas in a single `ToolSearch` call** using the `select:` syntax — e.g. `ToolSearch query="select:use_figma,get_screenshot,get_metadata,create_new_file"`. One round trip beats six.

**If the task involves building or updating a full page, screen, or multi-section layout in Figma from code**, also load [figma-generate-design](../figma-generate-design/SKILL.md). It provides the workflow for discovering design system components via `search_design_system`, importing them, and assembling screens incrementally. Both skills work together: this one for the API rules, that one for the screen-building workflow.

**If the task involves creating or building a component in Figma** (even a single component), also load [figma-generate-library](../figma-generate-library/SKILL.md). It provides the component creation workflow — variable foundations, variant sets, design token bindings — that `figma-use` alone doesn't cover.

Before anything, load [plugin-api-standalone.index.md](references/plugin-api-standalone.index.md) to understand what is possible. When you are asked to write plugin API code, use this context to grep [plugin-api-standalone.d.ts](references/plugin-api-standalone.d.ts) for relevant types, methods, and properties. This is the definitive source of truth for the API surface. It is a large typings file, so do not load it all at once, grep for relevant sections as needed.

IMPORTANT: Whenever you work with design systems, start with [working-with-design-systems/wwds.md](references/working-with-design-systems/wwds.md) to understand the key concepts, processes, and guidelines for working with design systems in Figma. Then load the more specific references for components, variables, text styles, and effect styles as needed.

## 1. Critical Rules

1.  **Use `return` to send data back.** The return value is JSON-serialized automatically (objects, arrays, strings, numbers). Do NOT call `figma.closePlugin()` or wrap code in an async IIFE — this is handled for you.
2.  **Write plain JavaScript with top-level `await` and `return`.** Code is automatically wrapped in an async context. Do NOT wrap in `(async () => { ... })()`.
3.  `figma.notify()` **throws "not implemented"** — never use it
3a. **Return node IDs and keep workflow state outside the Figma file.** Put human-readable component purpose and usage in the component's `description`.
4.  `console.log()` is NOT returned — use `return` for output
5.  **Work incrementally in small steps.** Break large operations into multiple `use_figma` calls. Validate after each step. This is the single most important practice for avoiding bugs.
6.  Colors are **0–1 range** (not 0–255): `{r: 1, g: 0, b: 0}` = red
7.  Fills/strokes are **read-only arrays** — clone, modify, reassign
8.  **Every text edit follows the canonical recipe: load font → `await` → mutate → return affected node IDs.** Skipping the load throws `Cannot write to node with unloaded font "<family> <style>"`. The rule covers more than `characters` — it applies to any operation on nodes with unloaded fonts (`appendChild`, `insertChild`, `setBoundVariable`, `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection`, `setValueForMode`, `findAll` callbacks touching text). When mutating existing text, load the node's *current* fonts via `getStyledTextSegments(['fontName'])`, not a hardcoded default. Inter is preloaded in most environments so other families surface this bug more often — the recipe is the same for every font. Use `await figma.listAvailableFontsAsync()` first if the style string is unverified. See [Canonical text-edit recipe](references/gotchas.md#canonical-text-edit-recipe-font-load--await--mutate--return-ids).
9.  **Pages load incrementally** — use `await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page)` to switch pages and load their content. The sync setter `figma.currentPage = page` does **NOT** work and will throw (see Page Rules below)
10. `setBoundVariableForPaint` returns a **NEW** paint — must capture and reassign
11. `createVariable` accepts collection **object or ID string** (object preferred)
12. **`layoutSizingHorizontal/Vertical` is value-restricted by structural context — `FIXED` always works, `HUG` and `FILL` do not.** `'HUG'` is valid only on an auto-layout frame itself OR on a **TEXT** child of one. `'FILL'` is valid only on a child of an auto-layout frame that is also not absolute-positioned, not inside an immutable frame, and not a canvas-grid child. Practical consequence: append to an auto-layout parent FIRST, then set `HUG`/`FILL` — a newly-created or unparented node can't satisfy the rule yet. The property itself exists on every `SceneNode`; the error is value-rejection, not "no such property". See [Gotchas](references/gotchas.md#layoutsizinghorizontallayoutsizingvertical-value-rules-fixed-hug-fill).
12a. **Use auto-layout for containers that hold related children.** When children have a structural relationship — stacked, side-by-side, aligned, gapped, hugged — wrap them in `figma.createAutoLayout()`, not `figma.createFrame()` with absolute `x`/`y`. Absolute coordinates govern where a container sits on the canvas; auto-layout governs how its children relate inside it. Skipping the container leaves no protection against text reflow, content changes, or overlap.
12b. **`layoutSizing*` and `*AxisSizingMode` are different enums — don't cross them.** `layoutSizingHorizontal`/`layoutSizingVertical` (set on a **child**) take `'FIXED'|'HUG'|'FILL'`; `primaryAxisSizingMode`/`counterAxisSizingMode` (set on the **frame** itself) take `'FIXED'|'AUTO'`. So `layoutSizingVertical = 'AUTO'` is invalid (use `'HUG'`), and `counterAxisSizingMode = 'FILL'` throws `Expected 'FIXED' | 'AUTO', received 'FILL'` (use `'FIXED'`/`'AUTO'`). Two more errors from the same setter — `Error: in set_layoutSizingHorizontal: node must be an auto-layout frame or a child of an auto-layout frame` and `Error: in set_layoutSizingHorizontal: FILL can only be set on children of auto-layout frames` — mean the node isn't in an auto-layout context yet; **recommendation: make the parent auto-layout (`figma.createAutoLayout()`) and `appendChild` the node before setting** (see Rule 12). See [Gotchas](references/gotchas.md#layoutsizing-vs-axissizingmode-two-different-sizing-enums).
13. **Position new top-level nodes away from (0,0).** Nodes appended directly to the page default to (0,0). Scan `figma.currentPage.children` to find a clear position (e.g., to the right of the rightmost node). This only applies to page-level nodes — nodes nested inside other frames or auto-layout containers are positioned by their parent. See [Gotchas](references/gotchas.md).
14. **On `use_figma` error, STOP. Do NOT immediately retry.** Failed scripts are **atomic** — if a script errors, it is not executed at all and no changes are made to the file. Read the error message carefully, fix the script, then retry. See [Error Recovery](#6-error-recovery--self-correction).
15. **MUST `return` ALL created/mutated node IDs.** Whenever a script creates new nodes or mutates existing ones on the canvas, collect every affected node ID and return them in a structured object (e.g. `return { createdNodeIds: [...], mutatedNodeIds: [...] }`). This is essential for subsequent calls to reference, validate, or clean up those nodes.
16. **Always set `variable.scopes` explicitly when creating variables.** The default `ALL_SCOPES` pollutes every property picker — almost never what you want. Use specific scopes like `["FRAME_FILL", "SHAPE_FILL"]` for backgrounds, `["TEXT_FILL"]` for text colors, `["GAP"]` for spacing, etc. See [variable-patterns.md](references/variable-patterns.md) for the full list.
17. **`await` every Promise.** Never leave a Promise unawaited — unawaited async calls (e.g. `figma.loadFontAsync(...)` without `await`, or `figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page)` without `await`) will fire-and-forget, causing silent failures or race conditions. The script may return before the async operation completes, leading to missing data or half-applied changes.
18. **Never read `componentPropertyDefinitions` from a variant component.** Narrow the owner first: use the node itself when it is a `COMPONENT_SET`, use a `COMPONENT` only when its parent is not a `COMPONENT_SET`, and otherwise promote a variant `COMPONENT` to its parent set. Optional chaining does not make the getter safe. See [Component-property owner narrowing](references/component-patterns.md#component-property-owner-narrowing).
> For detailed WRONG/CORRECT examples of each rule, see [Gotchas & Common Mistakes](references/gotchas.md).

## 2. Page Rules (Critical)

**Page context resets between `use_figma` calls** — `figma.currentPage` starts on the first page each time.

### Switching pages

Use `await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page)` to switch pages and load their content. The sync setter `figma.currentPage = page` does **NOT work** — it throws `"Setting figma.currentPage is not supported"` in `use_figma`. Always use the async method.

```js
// Switch to a specific page (loads its content)
const targetPage = figma.root.children.find((p) => p.name === "My Page");
await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(targetPage);
// targetPage.children is now populated
```

### Call `setCurrentPageAsync` at most once per `use_figma` invocation — fan multi-page work out in parallel

**One script must switch pages at most once.** Never loop over `figma.root.children` and switch pages inside the loop.

If the work spans multiple pages, **split it into N `use_figma` calls (one per target page) and emit them in parallel** — a single assistant message containing N `use_figma` tool-use blocks. The harness runs them concurrently; each script sets `currentPage` exactly once.

> **Explicit instruction:** when fanning out, you MUST issue the N tool calls in **one message**. Do not send them across multiple turns. Do not await one before issuing the next. Sequential per-page calls are slower than the in-loop pattern this rule replaces and waste the entire benefit of splitting.

```js
// AVOID — switches pages N times in one script, reloads the file each time
for (const page of figma.root.children) {
  await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page);
  // ... touch this page ...
}

// PREFER — read-only discovery call to get page IDs, then in the NEXT message
// emit N parallel use_figma tool calls (one per page), each setting currentPage once.
```

Default to parallel fan-out for any multi-page work — reads and writes alike. See [gotchas.md → Set current page once per `use_figma` call](references/gotchas.md#set-current-page-once-per-use_figma-call--split-multi-page-work-into-parallel-calls) for the full rationale.

### Across script runs

`figma.currentPage` resets to the **first page** at the start of each `use_figma` call. If your workflow spans multiple calls and targets a non-default page, call `await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page)` at the start of each invocation.

You can call `use_figma` multiple times to incrementally build on the file state, or to retrieve information before writing another script. For example, write a script to get metadata about existing nodes, `return` that data, then use it in a subsequent script to modify those nodes.

## 3. `return` Is Your Output Channel

The agent sees **ONLY** the value you `return`. Everything else is invisible.

- **Returning IDs (CRITICAL)**: Every script that creates or mutates canvas nodes **MUST** return all affected node IDs — e.g. `return { createdNodeIds: [...], mutatedNodeIds: [...] }`. This is a hard requirement, not optional.
- **Progress reporting**: `return { createdNodeIds: [...], count: 5, errors: [] }`
- **Error info**: Thrown errors are automatically captured and returned — just let them propagate or `throw` explicitly.
- `console.log()` output is **never** returned to the agent
- Always return actionable data (IDs, counts, status) so subsequent calls can reference created objects

## 4. Editor Mode

`use_figma` works in **design mode** (editorType `"figma"`, the default). FigJam (`"figjam"`) and Slides (`"slides"`) have different sets of available node types — most design nodes are blocked in FigJam, and FigJam-only nodes are blocked in Slides.

**Tell the editor from the URL:** Design = `figma.com/design/...`, FigJam = `figma.com/board/...`, Slides = `figma.com/slides/...`. Confirm before assuming an API is available.

Available in design mode: Rectangle, Frame, Component, Text, Ellipse, Star, Line, Vector, Polygon, BooleanOperation, Slice, Page, Section, TextPath.

**Blocked** in design mode: Sticky, Connector, ShapeWithText, CodeBlock, Slide, SlideRow, SlideGrid, InteractiveSlideElement, Webpage.

Available in Slides mode: Rectangle, Frame, Component, Text, Ellipse, Star, Line, Vector, Polygon, BooleanOperation, Slice, Section, TextPath, Slide, SlideRow, SlideGrid, InteractiveSlideElement.

**Blocked** in Slides mode: Sticky, Connector, ShapeWithText, CodeBlock, Webpage, Page.

**Design-only APIs (not just node types):** `figma.createPage()` is available only in Design files (`figma.com/design/...`). In both FigJam (`figma.com/board/...`) and Slides (`figma.com/slides/...`) it throws `TypeError: figma.createPage no such property 'createPage' on the figma global object`. Do not emit `figma.createPage()` in FigJam or Slides workflows.

> **Slides note:** There is no dedicated read tool for Slides files yet. Use `use_figma` with read-only scripts for inspection (see Section 6 "Inspect first" pattern), and `get_screenshot` / `await node.screenshot()` for visual context. For Slides-specific API guidance, load the [figma-use-slides](../figma-use-slides/SKILL.md) skill.

## 5. Efficient APIs — Prefer These Over Verbose Alternatives

These APIs reduce boilerplate, eliminate ordering errors, and compress token output. **Always prefer them over the verbose alternatives.**

### `node.query(selector)` — CSS-like node search

Find nodes within a subtree using CSS-like selectors. Replaces verbose `findAll` + filter loops.

```js
// BEFORE — verbose traversal
const texts = frame.findAll(n => n.type === 'TEXT' && n.name === 'Title')

// AFTER — one-liner with query
const texts = frame.query('TEXT[name=Title]')
```

**Selector syntax:**
- Type: `FRAME`, `TEXT`, `RECTANGLE`, `ELLIPSE`, `COMPONENT`, `INSTANCE`, `SECTION` (case-insensitive)
- Attribute exact: `[name=Card]`, `[visible=true]`, `[opacity=0.5]`
- Attribute substring: `[name*=art]` (contains), `[name^=Header]` (starts-with), `[name$=Nav]` (ends-with)
- Dot-path traversal: `[fills.0.type=SOLID]`, `[fills.*.type=SOLID]` (wildcard index)
- Instance matching: `[mainComponent=nodeId]`, `[mainComponent.name=Button]`
- Combinators: `FRAME > TEXT` (direct child), `FRAME TEXT` (any descendant), `A + B` (adjacent sibling), `A ~ B` (general sibling)
- Pseudo-classes: `:first-child`, `:last-child`, `:nth-child(2)`, `:not(TYPE)`, `:is(FRAME, RECTANGLE)`, `:where(TEXT, ELLIPSE)`
- Node ID: `#nodeId` or bare GUID
- Comma: `TEXT, RECTANGLE` (union)
- Wildcard: `*` (any type)

**QueryResult methods:**
| Method | Description |
|---|---|
| `.length` | Number of matched nodes |
| `.first()` | First matched node (or `null`) |
| `.last()` | Last matched node (or `null`) |
| `.toArray()` | Convert to regular array |
| `.each(fn)` | Iterate with callback, returns `this` for chaining |
| `.map(fn)` | Map to new array |
| `.filter(fn)` | Filter to new QueryResult |
| `.values(keys)` | Extract property values: `.values(['name', 'x', 'y'])` → `[{name, x, y}, ...]` |
| `.set(props)` | Set properties on all matched nodes (see `node.set()` below) |
| `.query(selector)` | Sub-query within matched nodes |
| `for...of` | Iterable — works in `for` loops |

**Scope:** `node.query()` searches within that node's subtree. To search the whole page: `figma.currentPage.query('...')`. There is no global `figma.query()`.

**Examples:**
```js
// Recolor all text inside cards
figma.currentPage.query('FRAME[name^=Card] TEXT').set({
  fills: [{type: 'SOLID', color: {r: 0.2, g: 0.2, b: 0.8}}]
})

// Get names and positions of all frames
return figma.currentPage.query('FRAME').values(['name', 'x', 'y'])

// Find the first component named "Button"
const btn = figma.currentPage.query('COMPONENT[name=Button]').first()

// Find all instances of a specific component
figma.currentPage.query(`INSTANCE[mainComponent=${compId}]`)

// Find nodes with solid fills using dot-path traversal
figma.currentPage.query('[fills.0.type=SOLID]')
```

### `node.set(props)` — batch property updates

Set multiple properties in one call. Returns `this` for chaining.

```js
// BEFORE — one line per property
frame.opacity = 0.5
frame.cornerRadius = 8
frame.name = "Card"

// AFTER — single call
frame.set({ opacity: 0.5, cornerRadius: 8, name: "Card" })
```

**Priority key ordering:** `layoutMode` is always applied before other properties (like `width`/`height`) regardless of object key order. This prevents the common bug where `resize()` behaves differently depending on whether `layoutMode` is set.

**Width/height handling:** `width` and `height` are routed through `node.resize()` automatically — setting `{ width: 200 }` calls `resize(200, currentHeight)`.

**Chaining with query:**
```js
// Find all rectangles named "Divider" and update them
figma.currentPage.query('RECTANGLE[name=Divider]').set({
  fills: [{type: 'SOLID', color: {r: 0.9, g: 0.9, b: 0.9}}],
  cornerRadius: 2
})
```

### `figma.createAutoLayout(direction?, props?)` — auto-layout frames

Creates a frame with auto-layout already enabled and both axes hugging content. **This is the default container whenever children have a structural relationship to each other (see Rule 12a).**

```js
// BEFORE — manual setup, easy to get ordering wrong
const frame = figma.createFrame()
frame.layoutMode = 'VERTICAL'
frame.primaryAxisSizingMode = 'AUTO'
frame.counterAxisSizingMode = 'AUTO'
frame.layoutSizingHorizontal = 'HUG'
frame.layoutSizingVertical = 'HUG'

// AFTER — one call, layout ready
const frame = figma.createAutoLayout('VERTICAL')
```

Children can immediately use `layoutSizingHorizontal/Vertical = 'FILL'` after being appended — no need to set sizing modes manually.

Accepts an optional props object as the first or second argument:
```js
figma.createAutoLayout({ name: 'Card', itemSpacing: 12 })               // HORIZONTAL + props
figma.createAutoLayout('VERTICAL', { name: 'Column', itemSpacing: 8 })  // VERTICAL + props
```

### `node.placeholder` — shimmer overlay for AI-in-progress feedback

Sets a visual shimmer overlay on a node indicating work is in progress. **Always remove the shimmer when done** — leftover shimmers confuse users and indicate incomplete work.

```js
// Mark as in-progress
frame.placeholder = true

// ... build out the content ...

// MUST remove when done — never leave shimmers on finished nodes
frame.placeholder = false
```

When building complex layouts, set `placeholder = true` on sections before populating them, then set `placeholder = false` on each section as it's completed.

### `await node.screenshot(opts?)` — inline screenshots

Capture a node as a PNG and return it inline in the response. Eliminates the need for a separate `get_screenshot` call.

```js
// Take a screenshot of a frame (returned inline in the tool response)
await frame.screenshot()

// Custom scale (default auto-scales: 0.5x or capped so max dimension ≤ 1024px)
await frame.screenshot({ scale: 2 })

// Include overlapping content from sibling nodes
await frame.screenshot({ contentsOnly: false })
```

**When to use:** After creating or modifying nodes, call `screenshot()` to visually verify the result within the same script. No need for a separate `get_screenshot` call.

**Auto-naming:** The image caption includes node metadata — `"Card (300x150 at 0,60).png"` — giving spatial context without parsing the image.

**Default scaling:** Uses 0.5x scale, but automatically caps so the largest output dimension never exceeds 1024px. Explicit `{ scale: N }` bypasses the cap.

## 6. Incremental Workflow (How to Avoid Bugs)

The most common cause of bugs is trying to do too much in a single `use_figma` call. **Work in small steps and validate after each one.**

### Key rules

- **At most 10 logical operations per `use_figma` call.** A "logical operation" is creating a node, setting its properties, and parenting it. If you need to create 20 nodes, split across 2-3 calls. **Slides override:** in Slides files, slides are isolated subtrees — the relevant limit is complexity per slide, not total nodes across slides. Building 3–5 new slides in one call is safe, and so is applying the same edit (e.g. adding a footer, recoloring a heading) across every slide in the deck in a single call. See [figma-use-slides](../figma-use-slides/SKILL.md) for the deck-building workflow.
- **Build top-down, starting with placeholders.** Create the outer structure first with `placeholder = true` on each section, then incrementally replace placeholders with real content in subsequent calls.

### The pattern

1. **Inspect first.** Before creating anything, run a read-only `use_figma` to discover what already exists in the file — pages, components, variables, naming conventions. Match what's there.
2. **Build the skeleton.** Create the top-level structure with placeholder sections. Set `placeholder = true` on each section so the user sees progress.
3. **Fill in sections incrementally.** In each subsequent call, populate one section and set its `placeholder = false` when done. Take a `screenshot()` to verify.
4. **Return IDs from every call.** Always `return` created node IDs, variable IDs, collection IDs as objects (e.g. `return { createdNodeIds: [...] }`). You'll need these as inputs to subsequent calls.
5. **Validate after each step.** Use `get_metadata` to verify structure (counts, names, hierarchy, positions). Use `await node.screenshot()` inline or `get_screenshot` after major milestones to catch visual issues.
6. **Fix before moving on.** If validation reveals a problem, fix it before proceeding to the next step. Don't build on a broken foundation.

### Suggested step order for complex tasks

```
Step 1: Inspect file — discover existing pages, components, variables, conventions
Step 2: Create tokens/variables (if needed)
       → validate with get_metadata
Step 3: Create individual components
       → validate with get_metadata + get_screenshot
Step 4: Compose layouts from component instances
       → validate with get_screenshot
Step 5: Final verification
```

### What to validate at each step

| After... | Check with `get_metadata` | Check with `get_screenshot` |
|---|---|---|
| Creating variables | Collection count, variable count, mode names | — |
| Creating components | Child count, variant names, property definitions | Variants visible, not collapsed, grid readable |
| Binding variables | Node properties reflect bindings | Colors/tokens resolved correctly |
| Composing layouts | Instance nodes have mainComponent, hierarchy correct | No cropped/clipped text, no overlapping elements, correct spacing |

## 7. Error Recovery & Self-Correction

**`use_figma` is atomic — failed scripts do not execute.** If a script errors, no changes are made to the file. The file remains in the same state as before the call. This means there are no partial nodes, no orphaned elements from the failed script, and retrying after a fix is safe.

### When `use_figma` returns an error

1. **STOP.** Do not immediately fix the code and retry.
2. **Read the error message carefully.** Understand exactly what went wrong — wrong API usage, missing font, invalid property value, etc.
3. **If the error is unclear**, call `get_metadata` or `get_screenshot` to understand the current file state.
4. **Fix the script** based on the error message.
5. **Retry** the corrected script.

### Common self-correction patterns
| Error message | Likely cause | How to fix |
|---|---|---|
| `"not implemented"` | Used `figma.notify()` | Remove it — use `return` for output |
| `Error: in set_layoutSizingHorizontal: node must be an auto-layout frame or a child of an auto-layout frame` / `Error: in set_layoutSizingHorizontal: FILL can only be set on children of auto-layout frames` / `"HUG can only be set on auto-layout frames or text children of auto-layout frames"` / `"FILL cannot be set on absolute positioned auto-layout children"` / `"FILL cannot be set on canvas grid children"` | Tried to assign `HUG`/`FILL` to a node whose structural context doesn't allow it (e.g. parent isn't auto-layout, ran before `appendChild`, non-text child trying to `HUG`, absolute-positioned child trying to `FILL`) | Make the parent auto-layout via `figma.createAutoLayout()`; `appendChild` first; reserve `HUG` for the auto-layout frame itself or for TEXT children; for absolute/immutable/grid children use `FIXED` + `resize()`. See [gotchas.md](references/gotchas.md#layoutsizinghorizontallayoutsizingvertical-value-rules-fixed-hug-fill) |
| `"Setting figma.currentPage is not supported"` | Used sync page setter (`figma.currentPage = page`) which does NOT work | Use `await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page)` — the only way to switch pages |
| `Error: in get_componentPropertyDefinitions: Can only get component property definitions of a component set or non-variant component` | Read `componentPropertyDefinitions` from a variant `COMPONENT` | Read from its parent `COMPONENT_SET` instead. Narrow the owner before touching the getter; optional chaining does not prevent this error. See [component-property owner narrowing](references/component-patterns.md#component-property-owner-narrowing). |
| Property value out of range | Color channel > 1 (used 0–255 instead of 0–1) | Divide by 255 |
| `"Cannot read properties of null"` | Node doesn't exist (wrong ID, wrong page) | Check page context, verify ID |
| Script hangs / no response | Infinite loop or unresolved promise | Check for `while(true)` or missing `await`; ensure code terminates |
| `"The node with id X does not exist"` | Parent instance was implicitly detached by a child `detachInstance()`, changing IDs | Re-discover nodes by traversal from a stable (non-instance) parent frame |

### When the script succeeds but the result looks wrong

1. Call `get_metadata` to check structural correctness (hierarchy, counts, positions).
2. Call `get_screenshot` to check visual correctness. Look closely for cropped/clipped text (line heights cutting off content) and overlapping elements — these are common and easy to miss.
3. Identify the discrepancy — is it structural (wrong hierarchy, missing nodes) or visual (wrong colors, broken layout, clipped content)?
4. Write a targeted fix script that modifies only the broken parts — don't recreate everything.

> For the full validation workflow, see [Validation & Error Recovery](references/validation-and-recovery.md).

## 8. Pre-Flight Checklist

Before submitting ANY `use_figma` call, verify:
- [ ] Code uses `return` to send data back (NOT `figma.closePlugin()`)
- [ ] Code is NOT wrapped in an async IIFE (auto-wrapped for you)
- [ ] `return` value includes structured data with actionable info (IDs, counts)
- [ ] NO usage of `figma.notify()` anywhere
- [ ] NO usage of `console.log()` as output (use `return` instead)
- [ ] All colors use 0–1 range (not 0–255)
- [ ] Paint `color` objects use `{r, g, b}` only — no `a` field (opacity goes at the paint level: `{ type: 'SOLID', color: {...}, opacity: 0.5 }`)
- [ ] Fills/strokes are reassigned as new arrays (not mutated in place)
- [ ] Page switches use `await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page)` (sync setter `figma.currentPage = page` does NOT work)
- [ ] `layoutSizingVertical/Horizontal = 'FILL'` is set AFTER `parent.appendChild(child)`
- [ ] Wrapping TEXT blocks set `textAutoResize = 'HEIGHT'` and an explicit width (`'FIXED'` + `resize()`) — NOT `FILL` alone, which the default `WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT` mode ignores, collapsing the node to a near-zero-width thread. Verify `node.width > 0`
- [ ] Every text mutation follows the [canonical recipe](references/gotchas.md#canonical-text-edit-recipe-font-load--await--mutate--return-ids): `loadFontAsync` → `await` → mutate `characters`/font/size/etc. → return affected node IDs. Works for ANY font family/style, not just Inter (which only happens to be preloaded).
- [ ] Style names have already been verified via `listAvailableFontsAsync()` — NOT guessed from memory (`"SemiBold"` vs `"Semi Bold"` is a common footgun)
- [ ] For `FONT_FAMILY`-scoped variables: every value across every relevant mode is loaded before `setBoundVariable("fontFamily", …)`, `setValueForMode`, or `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection`
- [ ] `lineHeight`/`letterSpacing` use `{unit, value}` format (not bare numbers)
- [ ] `resize()` is called BEFORE setting sizing modes (resize resets them to FIXED)
- [ ] Every `componentPropertyDefinitions` read is performed only after narrowing to a `COMPONENT_SET` or a non-variant `COMPONENT`; variant components are promoted to their parent set first
- [ ] For multi-step workflows: IDs from previous calls are passed as string literals (not variables)
- [ ] New top-level nodes are positioned away from (0,0) to avoid overlapping existing content
- [ ] Containers with structurally-related children use `figma.createAutoLayout()`, not absolute x/y (see Rule 12a)
- [ ] ALL created/mutated node IDs are collected and included in the `return` value
- [ ] Every async call (`loadFontAsync`, `setCurrentPageAsync`, `importComponentByKeyAsync`, etc.) is `await`ed — no fire-and-forget Promises

## 9. Discover Conventions Before Creating

**Always inspect the Figma file before creating anything.** Different files use different naming conventions, variable structures, and component patterns. Your code should match what's already there, not impose new conventions.

When in doubt about any convention (naming, scoping, structure), check the Figma file first, then the user's codebase. Only fall back to common patterns when neither exists.

### Quick inspection scripts

**List all pages and top-level nodes:**
```js
const pages = figma.root.children.map(p => `${p.name} id=${p.id} children=${p.children.length}`);
return pages.join('\n');
```

**List existing components across all pages:**

`search_design_system` is an option for published components. For on-canvas components, use the two-step fan-out — **don't loop pages inside one script.**

Step 1: one read-only `use_figma` to get page IDs:
```js
return figma.root.children.map(p => ({ id: p.id, name: p.name }));
```

Step 2: in the **next assistant turn, emit one `use_figma` per page in parallel** (a single message containing N tool-use blocks). Each runs:
```js
const page = await figma.getNodeByIdAsync(PAGE_ID);
await figma.setCurrentPageAsync(page);
// findAllWithCriteria uses an indexed type lookup — hundreds of times faster
// than the findAll(n => n.type === '…') side-effect-in-predicate antipattern.
const matches = page.findAllWithCriteria({ types: ['COMPONENT', 'COMPONENT_SET'] });
return matches.map(n => ({ page: page.name, name: n.name, type: n.type, id: n.id }));
```

**List existing variable collections and their conventions:**
```js
const collections = await figma.variables.getLocalVariableCollectionsAsync();
const results = collections.map(c => ({
  name: c.name, id: c.id,
  varCount: c.variableIds.length,
  modes: c.modes.map(m => m.name)
}));
return results;
```

## ASSISTANT
Both are in the file, just right of Section 1 — search the layers panel for **`PROPOSAL A`** and **`PROPOSAL B`**.

## How to read them

Each frame shows **all three animation states at once**, so one still tells you what the motion does:

- **Row 1, green at half opacity** — a step that's finished
- **Row 2, teal, bold, with the bar on the left** — happening now
- **Row 3, ghosted grey** — not yet

So animating it is just moving that treatment down the list, one row per VO phrase — *decomposes* → row 1, *evaluates the result* → row 2, *repeats until the condition is met* → row 3. The log in both is frozen at the same moment as the live row: step 2 has just failed.

## A — keep the monitor

Caption moved up to `y=52`, which frees the band at `258–448` for the stack. Everything else untouched: same plate, same monitor, same log position, same screen rect. **Nothing needs re-exporting from Figma and nothing changes in `spec.py` except the icon block.**

The trade is size. The band is 190px, so glyphs land at 44px — *smaller* than the 124px rings you have now. It wins on legibility because it's labelled and sequenced, not because it's bigger.

## B — push in

The screen becomes the frame. That buys 480px for the stack, so glyphs go to 110px and labels to 37px, with the log as a dense band below the rule at `y=846`.

This is the version that actually answers "presentation template." It's also the one that holds up at thumbnail size and on a phone held at arm's length. The cost is a new plate and a new panel in the timeline — it's a real shot, not an overlay.

## Which I'd pick

**B, and I don't think it's close.** A is better than what's there, but it's still a small thing squeezed into a gap, and the criticism was that the visuals didn't carry the story — 44px glyphs won't carry it either. B is a push-in to a screen the character is already staring at, which is a normal comic move, and it's the only version where the three stages are the subject of the frame rather than decoration above it.

It also keeps the thing worth protecting: the log stays dense and unreadable at the bottom, so the panel is still over your head. Structure, not simplification.

## One thing to look at before deciding

In B, row 3 sits at 34% opacity. On this screen it reads fine; on a phone in daylight it may vanish. Easy to lift to 45% — but check it on your phone first, because if pending states are too visible the "now" stops being obvious, and that contrast is the whole mechanism.

Say which one and I'll wire it into `spec.py` and animate it. If it's B I'll also need to know whether it replaces frame 15 or sits as an extra panel after it — replacing is tighter, adding costs about a second.

Six genuinely different representations, not layout variants. For each: what's on screen, and what the failure beat looks like — because that's the beat that teaches.

## 1 · The bar that goes backwards

One bar. Nothing else. `0 → 1/3 → 2/3 →` **back to 1/3** `→ 2/3 → 3/3`.

The reversal *is* the definition. "Evaluates the result" means the check can send you back, and a bar losing ground says that in one frame with no words at all. Most legible thing per pixel on this list, readable as a thumbnail, and it's about six lines of code.

The risk is that it's *too* clean — it might read as a download, not as reasoning.

## 2 · A loop that closes

Three boxes down the frame, an arrow returning up the right edge. The return arrow only draws itself on "repeats until the condition is met", so the shape completes on the word.

`comic-animation.md` says demonstrate, don't diagram — but a loop is one of the few things where the diagram *is* the mechanism. Vertical format suits it, and the closing edge is a real event rather than a state change.

## 3 · A checklist that un-ticks itself

Three to-dos. Item 1 ticks. Item 2 ticks — **then the tick scrubs itself out** — then re-ticks. Item 3 ticks.

The un-tick is the strongest single beat available, because nobody expects a checklist to go backwards. It's also faintly funny, which is on-brief. Cheap: three rows of text and a tick that animates out.

## 4 · Tickets through a stamp

Three tickets feed through a stamp. Two come out `OK`. One comes out `REJECTED` and drops back onto the top of the pile.

This rhymes with `LOOP · CLEARED` — the series already ends on a stamp, so using one here makes the visual language feel deliberate rather than assembled per panel. Physical, countable, and the rejected ticket rejoining the pile is unmistakable.

## 5 · Him versus it, side by side

Two columns. **Left:** a person doing step-check-step by hand. **Right:** the same three steps running on their own. The left column stops — he goes home — and the right column keeps going.

This is the only option that does *three* jobs: it explains the loop, it sets up "nobody in the middle" two panels later, and it pre-loads the credits punchline, because the thing that kept running is the thing that spent the money. Best story value on the list, and the most expensive — it needs art, not shapes.

## 6 · The mark does it all

No new iconography. The AI mark splits into three petal-groups (decomposes), spins (repeats), squints on the failure, then widens and turns green (condition met).

Cheapest of all — the split and the spin already exist in the code — and it's the most on-brand, since one body for the AI is a locked rule. But it's abstract, and abstract is exactly what your manager didn't read the first time.

---

## What I'd actually do

**#3 or #4, and put #1 underneath it as the counter.** A checklist or a ticket queue gives you objects that fail visibly, and a single bar along the bottom gives the eye one countable thing to follow. Two layers, two jobs, neither competing — which is what went wrong with three equal rings in a row.

**Don't** reach for a friendly metaphor here — a vending machine, a rabbit, a conveyor belt. That's the *plain* explanation's job, and the dot-and-finish-line already has it. If this panel becomes charming, the pivot two panels later has nothing to pivot from. This one stays dense; it just needs to be dense **and** organised.

Say two and I'll build them in Figma next to A and B, same three-states-in-one-frame trick so you can judge the motion from a still.

Each one shows the whole motion in a single still — a finished state, a live state, and a not-yet state — so you can read the animation without me building it.

## What surprised me building them

**1 is the strongest teach and I didn't expect that.** A bar losing ground needs no words, no icons and no legend. Five snapshots down the frame and the reversal is the loudest thing in the panel. It's also the only option that would still read on a 200px thumbnail.

**5 stopped being expensive.** I'd priced it as needing character art; done as two columns of step slots it needs none, and it's still the only option that pays off three separate beats — the loop, "nobody in the middle", and the credits. The `6:04pm / 11:20pm` pair does the work.

**6 is prettier than it is useful.** The marks came out well — those are the real petal geometry, generated from the same numbers as `aimark.py`, so they can't drift from the video. But it's still three abstract blooms with labels beside them, which is close to the thing your manager already didn't read. I'd park it.

**2 is the most correct and the least alive.** It's an accurate flowchart. It's also the one that most looks like a slide someone made in a hurry, and the red return edge is doing all the work.

## If I had to choose

**5 as the panel, with 1 as the layer inside it.** The two columns carry the story, and a bar under each column gives the eye the one countable thing to follow. That combination is the only one that fixes the original complaint — *I didn't get the story* — because it actually has a story in it, rather than a better-labelled diagram.

Second choice is **3**, purely because the un-tick is the single best beat on the board and it's almost free to animate.

**One caveat on all eight:** the vertical spacing is mock spacing, not final. Several have a dead bottom third, and if you pick one I'd redistribute before animating. I didn't tune it because it would have been eight lots of tuning for seven frames you're going to discard.

## ASSISTANT
In the file as `PLAIN 1 / 2 / 3`, one row below the technical set.

**These are staging mocks, not artwork.** The rabbit is four ellipses; the kettle is a rounded rectangle. What they're testing is composition, scale and what moves — the real versions come out of ChatGPT with the cast sheet attached.

## 1 · The robot vacuum

Top-down room. It's mid-job, the path behind it shows where it's been, a red dot marks where it hit a chair leg and went round, and it returns to the dock on its own. Counter reads `2 of 3 rooms`.

Closest one-to-one mapping on the list — it works unattended, it **re-plans on an obstacle**, and it stops itself. It's also the only one that covers "re-plans", which the current dot-and-flag doesn't attempt.

It's also a gift for the script: *"left the vacuum running"* is nearly the same sentence as *"left a loop running."*

## 2 · The kettle

The single best object for "termination condition" — everyone alive knows a kettle switches itself off, and nobody has ever stood watching one. It also hands the audio a free beat: the **click**.

But it has no repetition and no failure. It gives you *stops itself* and nothing else, which is one-third of the line.

## 3 · The rabbit and the basket — yours

Your idea from earlier, staged as three beats down the frame: hop, lift the lid, empty · hop, lift the lid, still empty · hop, **carrot, it stops**.

Of the three this is the one I didn't expect to like most. Three beats stacked vertically uses the format properly, the hop is countable, lifting the lid *is* the check made physical, and the carrot appearing is a genuine payoff rather than a state change. It's also the only one that's funny.

## What I'd do

**3, and steal the vacuum's failure beat.** The rabbit gives you repetition, a visible check and a payoff. What it's missing is the re-plan — so on beat 2 the rabbit tries the wrong basket, or the lid sticks. That covers the full line and keeps the charm.

**The kettle is better used elsewhere.** It's too good an object to waste as a third of an explanation — it's a whole episode's worth of "termination condition" if a later term needs one.

## Two things this changes

**The emoji disintegration still works, and it gets better.** Whatever replaces the dot, there's still a human to remove on *"nobody in the middle"* — and removing someone from beside a rabbit is funnier than removing a floating head from beside a dot.

**Scale is the real fix regardless of which you pick.** The current panel puts the whole mechanism in about 180px of a 1280px frame. All three mocks use roughly half the frame, and that alone will do more than the change of metaphor.

Tell me the pick and I'll wire up `2` for the technical panel and this one for the plain, plus the spacing pass I owe you on both.
